approval. The State agency will maintain all necessary supporting data for these expenditures for review upon request.

Effective on and after October 31, 1963, the cost to the State of land necessary to the construction or purchase of a publicly owned office building, used in the provision of office space to a State or local public assistance agency, may be included in the total costs of such building and claimed for Federal financial participation in the form of rental charges during the use-life of the building or during an acceptable shorter amortization period. 

The amount of land cost to be included would be based on the proportion of space occupied by the agency in the building. An adjustment would be required of the Federal financial participation in the cost of the land at any time that the building located thereon becomes unavailable to the State agency (see V-4532.51).

The State agency would have the options to adjust for the amount determined to represent the Federal financial interest in the land by a lump sum or an installment decrease in its grant computation over a reasonable period of time, or a decrease in the claim for other acquired agency office space.

For the cost of land to be included for Federal financial participation, the State or local public assistance agency must be an original occupant of the newly constructed or newly purchased office building; and in the instance of a constructed building, the land must have been acquired not more than 3 years prior to the initiation of construction, unless a longer period can be justified.

Where a State agency is currently claiming rental charges based upon construction or purchase of an office building and it meets the conditions of the amended policy, its plan of rental charges may, for periods on and after October 31, 1963, the effective date of the policy, be amended to include a percentage of the land cost which bears the same ratio as the then unexpired amortization period bears to the total of that period. Such portion of the land cost may be added by either increasing, within limits of comparable rent, the rental charges for the unexpired amortization period; or by extending sufficiently the period of amortization to include the cost of land.

Parking Facilities 

The costs of providing parking facilities necessary for the efficient administration of the public assistance programs will be subject to Federal financial participation. Such costs must be permissible under State law, reasonable, and incurred in providing facilities not otherwise available. 

The provision of parking facilities for the use of the staff of the State and local public assistance agencies, and for 

H.T. No. 20 7) !

Part V 

4000-4999 4550. 4551. 4552.

  Administrative Expenditures 

12/31/63 

Parking Facilities (Continued) 

persons who may need to visit the offices on official business, is an important means of facilitating the administration of the public assistance programs. Since home investigations of applicants and recipients of public assistance are generally necessary in the determination of eligibility, it is usual that such investigations are made by field workers using a car. In view of the location of many local public assistance agencies in business districts where streets in the vicinity of the offices are congested and of the widespread practice of metering of street space in urban localities, it becomes increasingly important for field workers as well as other agency staff to have ready access to convenient parking space. For the same reasons, some provision for off-street parking is frequently necessary for the public that may need to visit the public assistance office on official business. 

Requirements for State Plans 

To secure Federal financial participation in the costs of providing parking facilities, the State public assistance agency must include in its plan the following: 

1. All pertinent State rules and regulations governing State agency expenditures for parking facilities. (These may be identified, cited, and briefly summarized.) 

2. The State agency's policies governing the cost of parking facilities including its procedures for the review and approval of any such costs incurred by the local public assistance agencies.

Federal Financial Participation 

Federal financial participation is available in State agency expenses of providing parking facilities under the general principles applicable to all administrative costs (V-4020).

Participation in such expenses would include the costs of providing parking facilities of benefit to both the staff of the public assistance agency and to persons who may need to visit the office of the public assistance agency on official business.

Office Space 

Criteria for the Administration of the Plan (continued) 3. Consultation is provided to local offices in planning for meeting the required State standards . 4.

Action is taken to secure improvement of State and local office space, equipment, and facilities when they are found to be below established standards.

Interpretation 

In the performance of its function of administering or supervising the administration of the public assistance programs, the State agency has the responsibility for assuring that there is adequate housing designed for the specialized welfare functions in serving its particular clientele. The kind of housing, office space layout, equipment, and facilities directly affect the quality of administration of the public assistance programs and effectiveness of the staff in providing services to the public. 

In establishing and maintaining standards for office space, equipment, and facilities, attention needs to be given to making the agency office as attractive as possible, whereby cleanliness, lighting, color, and furnishings enhance the overall appearance and promote community respect for the public welfare office, efficient work flow, dignity of and privacy for the client, and the morale and productivity of agency staff. Special attention must be given also to transportation facilities to and from the office, including parking facilities for both clients and staff.

4610. Protective Services 4611. 

Public Liability Costs.-- 4611.32 Federal Financial Participation.-The Social Security Administra tion considers as an expenditure proper to the administration of the public assistance programs, the payment of the State agency's share of the cost of protecting members of the public against injury to person or property occasioned by the operation of privately or publicly owned motor vehicles by its employees in the discharge of the State agency's official business. The costs of premiums for public liability or property damage insurance, and the awards, judgments, or settlements arising from injury to person or property under the above-mentioned circumstances will be regarded as administrative expenditures in which Federal matching may be claimed,

With respect to State and local employees engaged in the administration of the public assistance programs, the cost incident to any one, or a combination, of the following method of protection, State law permitting, is considered a proper administrative expenditure in which Federal matching is available: 

1. Liability or property damage insurance; 2. 3.

Waiver of sovereign immunity and suit against the State itself; 

Presentation of claims before a State examining board, or other statutory body having authority to make or approve a final award or settlement.

After determining the method for the protection of the public against personal or property injury, the State agencies will need to be guided by certain conditions in submitting claims for 

Federal participation in the administrative costs incurred: 

1. Claims for Federal participation shall include only those costs arising out of conduct of the State agency's official business; 

2. Where public liability or property damage insurance is utilized and such insurance covers other agencies of the State, including the State public assistance agency, the 177

4630. 4633.2 

Bank Service Charges on Public Assistance Payment Accounts. 

4/14/47 

Federal Financial Participation.--Where other arrangements can not be made, the Social Security Administration considers as an expenditure proper to the administration of the public assistance programs the payment of charges required by a bank for handling the account against which checks are issued by either a State or local agency for public assistance payments. This applies to assistance payments only and is not applicable to checks issued in payment of the administrative expenses of the agency, because it is the monthly volume of assistance checks, in the absence of a compensating balance, which sets the public welfare agency account apart from other State accounts. 

When incurring expenditures for bank service charges, the State agencies will need to consider the following criteria that will govern Federal participation in such administrative costs: 1. 

Federal matching will be available only when payments made by either the State or local agencies to the banks for handling their public assistance accounts are necessary and reasonable under the particular circumstances, and are permitted by State law. 

2. Expenditures made by the local administrative unit have received the approval of the State agency. 

Under these criteria, the State is expected to make every effort to work out some plan whereby such charges would be avoided, which would include consideration of the feasibility of using other banking facilities that might not require service charges. 

The rate of any service charge is expected to be commensurate with both the volume of checks handled each month and the average balance of State-appropriated and Federal funds maintained in this account, as well as State and county funds in other accounts in the same bank, This applies whether the charge is paid on a rate-per-check basis, or as a flat monthly amount, if the agency and the bank agree on the latter method. 

This policy was effective on and after October 1, 1945.

Costs of State Agency Memberships in Organizations.--Matching under titles I, IV, and X, of the Social Security Act will be available for the cost of State and local agency memberships in organizations providing services for the advancement of health, welfare, and community organization activities, including merit system agency membership in organizations providing services for the advancement of personnel administration, such costs to be deemed necessary for proper and efficient administration when the following conditions have been met: 1. 2. 3. 4. 5. 6.

The expenditure is permissible under State law; 

The expenditure is only for agency memberships, not individual memberships;

The membership is in a nonprofit international, national, State or local organization;

The services provided are reasonably related to the administration of the particular program; 

The cost of the membership is reasonably related to the value of the services or benefits received; and

The expenditure is not for membership in an organization which devotes a substantial part of its activities to influencing legislation.

 Administrative Expenditures Surveys and Studies by Private Agencies or Firms 4650.2 Federal Financial Participation 

Federal financial participation is available in expenditures for surveys and studies of the public assistance programs by private agencies or firms, subject to the applicable principles and requirements pertaining to administrative costs included in Handbook V-4030 and V-4110. The cost of such surveys and studies is matchable only when they are performed under the control of the public assistance agency when they serve the needs and objective of these programs, and when the costs do not include other Federal funds. Survey activities include: organization, management and position classification surveys, and studies of program operations. 

See "Green Sheet's'

V-G-4000 

Private Funds In relation to the financing of the survey or study, State and local funds may include those derived from private sources, such as contributions by private organizations or individuals. These contributions must be deposited in the account of the State or local public assistance agency in accordance with State law for expenditures by and at the sole discretion of the State agency, or by and at the sole discretion of the local agency subject only to the supervision of the State agency. No additional requirement to define funds or limit the purposes may be imposed. 

For example, no contribution may be treated as State funds for matching purposes which is intended for the employment of an individual or firm designated by the contributor. To the extent, also, that contributions may revert to the donor's facility or use, they may not be used for matching purposes. 

Request for Federal Matching Approval before the initiation of a study is not necessary. However, such prior approval provides certain safeguards to the State with regard to Federal matching. 

The State must submit a written request for approval prior to the claim for Federal participation. Such approval will be given after a finding that the cost of such a study or survey is necessary for the proper and efficient operation of the State's public assistance program. The State's request shall contain the following information:

4650.3 Recommendations 6 The Bureau is interested in all types of surveys, studies, and methods which may improve the administration of the public assistance programs.

Its staff will work with a State agency in evaluating the need for a survey or study and in planning for the utilization of appropriate facilities, which might be those of the Department of Health, Education, and Welfare, those available from other public departments within a State, or the services of a private agency. However, surveys and studies are not within themselves a panacea for administrative problems. 

They are only methods for detecting causative factors and arriving at recommended remedial measures.

The Bureau of Public Assistance is often able to provide technical assistance to State agencies in these areas. Such help may consist of (1) providing consultation and information with respect to problems encountered, (2) providing assistance to the State agency in conducting its own survey or study, or (3) conducting the survey or study and providing the State agency with a report of findings and recommendations. Therefore, a State agency when contemplating need for a survey or study may wish to ascertain from the appropriate regional office whether help will be available from this source which will meet the specific problem or need. !!

4000-4999 4660-4661.1 2/20/50 

 Administrative Expenditures Management Services (continued) 

4660. 4661. 4661.1 

Audits.-It is the responsibility of the State public assistance agency to establish and maintain internal accounting, statistical, fiscal, and administrative controls in order to insure proper accountability for the expenditure of Federal and State funds. The making of audits is one means of maintaining and testing the adequacy of such controls.

A continuing administrative relationship should exist between the State public assistance agency and the State auditing department in order that the State auditor may be satisfied that the internal controls insure proper handling of the funds. 

If, in addition to the audits and other internal controls maintained as part of the public assistance agency's responsibility, the State wishes to make further audit of the agency's accounts, it is assumed that such audits are made for the State's own purposes and therefore would not be matched by Federal funds.

Pre-audits by another agency raise questions of conformity with the single State agency requirement of the Social Security Act unless limited to necessary accounting verifications of accuracy and of compliance with State law and regulation, so that they do not infringe upon the State agency's authority to administer and supervise the public assistance programs. 

Under the requirements of the Social Security Act, an authority separate from the State public assistance agency, within the governmental jurisdiction, cannot be empowered in the plan, nor permitted in practice to substitute its judgment for that of the responsible public assistance agency in carrying out the purposes of the public assistance laws. Requirements Requirements for State plans.--The State public assistance plan must describe how the responsibility for maintaining proper fiscal controls is carried out through the audit process; the types of audits, regular and recurring, or special; the kinds of audits, test checks or 100 percent; the purpose, scope, and coverage of each type of audit; the use to be made of the audit report; and whether the audits are to be performed by the State public assistance agency or by a firm of private auditors employed by the public assistance agency.

Part V. 

6667-0007

4661.2 2/20/50 Federal financial participation.--The cost of audits performed by or under the exclusive control of the State public assistance agency, that are essential as a part of the State agency's procedures in maintaining a system of internal controls for handling public assistance funds, will be matched. 

If the audit is performed by a private firm of auditors at the request of the public assistance agency, the State public assistance agency must be responsible for the final determination or approval of the type of audit to be made, its scope and purpose, the availability and use of the audit findings, and the distribution of the report. ​4700. Preattack Planning for Emergency Welfare Preparedness 

4710. Purpose

The purpose of this policy is to stimulate the preattack development of emergency welfare readiness programs, include staff training in the operation of such programs, and provide for cooperation with other government and voluntary agencies in civil defense test exercises. Further, this policy is intended to increase the opportunity for Federal and State cooperation in finding new approaches and methods for assuring the continuation of public assistance programs under disaster conditions. 

4720. Federal Financial Participation 

Federal financial participation is available in the following personal service and other costs which are chargeable to funds expended under titles I, IV, X, XIV, and XVI of the Social 

Security Act: 

1. 2. 3. 4. 5.

The preservation and protection of essential documents and records and the production of duplicate records; 

Planning and developing emergency operating capability for present programs, including related utilization of emergency programs which will provide social services, feeding, lodging, clothing, and welfare registration and inquiry; 

Assuring that qualified individuals are responsible for the planning and operation of each welfare function essential under emergency conditions for care and services for public assistance recipients and potential recipients; 

Cooperating with other government and voluntary welfare agencies, and welfare-related business and professional organizations and associations, in developing emergency operating plans and attaining operational readiness; 

Preparing and maintaining data on kinds, mmbers, and locations of essential welfare resources, including manpower;

H.T. No. 44 

191

- p.2 

4700-4799 4720.

12/18/64 

Federal Financial Participation (Continued) 

6. 7. 8. 9.

Developing ability to assess postattack welfare resources and determining requirements necessary to care for public assistance cases;

Preparing plans for claimancy and distribution of the above resources;

Developing mutual aid agreements at State and local levels with neighboring welfare organizations; 

Preparing written emergency operations plans for public assistance agencies and operating units which provide for maximum use of all resources, and are integrated into the overall community organization plan for operations; distributing the plans to all operating units, and assuring that staff understands the actions to take in the event of an emergency; 

10. Providing training in emergency operations for all staff and for volunteers whose services will be needed for operations under emergency conditions; 11.

Participating in civil defense exercises for the purpose of testing plans and determining the role of public assistance programs in relation to the overall civil defense program; and 

12. Travel incidental to any of the above activities.

For the public assistance titles, the above defense readiness activities are necessary to assure continuity of the public assistance programs under disaster conditions, and costs incident to such activities are part of the overall administrative expenses chargeable to programs under titles I, IV, X, XIV, and XVI of the Social Security Act, and may therefore be included in total in expenses distributable among these programs.

Certification of Eligible Households for Food Coupons or Surplus 

Food Distribution

The Food Stamp Act of 1964 (see Handbook Part I-1300, 12/30/64) 

provides that a State, at its request, may obtain through the Department of Agricultrue an approved food stamp program, to be operated by the State or local agency having the responsibility for the administration of the federally aided public assistance programs. The Food Stamp Act does not abolish the surplus food direct distribution program of the Agriculture Marketing Service.

It does, however, prohibit the distribution of surplus food to needy households in those areas in which the food stamp program is in effect, except in an emergent situation, as determined by the Secretary of Agriculture. (The distribution of surplus food to non-profit school lunch programs and eligible institutions will continue, however, in those areas in which the food stamp program is in effect.)

Federal Financial Participation

B. Federal financial participation is not available for matching 

State or local welfare agency expenditures for the certification of households in which no members are public assistance recipients. C.

1000-5999 

Statistical Reporting and Research Objectives and Methods 

10/23/64 

3000. Objectives and Methods 

3100. Objectives

The statistical reporting and research program of the Bureau is designed to produce information for use in the Department of Health, Education, and Welfare in: 

1. Reporting to the Congress of the United States on the status of operations under public assistance and related titles of the Social Security Act, and estimating the amount of Federal funds required for grants to State under these titles; 

2. Evaluating the effectiveness of existing Federal statutory provisions for public assistance, and preparing recommendations concerning desirable modifications and extensions of such provisions; 

3. Developing interpretations of the requirements of the Social 

Security Act for State plans for public assistance programs and for the administration of such programs; 

4. Developing recommended standards and procedures for public assistance programs, for consideration by State public assistance agencies; 

5. Evaluating the effectiveness of State plans for public assistance programs and the administration of such programs; 6. and

Providing information concerning public assistance programs in the United States to interested public and private agencies and individuals.

In developing and carrying out its statistical reporting and research program, the Bureau is guided by the principle that this program shall, to the fullest extent possible, serve the needs both of the Bureau and of State agencies in meeting needs for information for use in reporting and making legislative recommendations, preparing budget requests, planning and evaluating program operation, formulating policy, developing standards and procedures, and providing public information.

H.T. No. 36

Part VI 

1000-5999 

Statistical Reporting and Research Objectives and Methods 

10/23/64 

3200. Methods of Collecting Information 

Two methods of collecting information are utilized in carrying out the statistical reporting and research program of the Bureau: 

1. Periodic Reports 

Periodic reports cover items of information which must be, or are preferably, collected on a periodic basis to be of maximum usefulness, and the collection of which on such a basis is practicable in terms of staff effort and cost, 2. Special Studies and One-time Reports 

Special studies are designed to produce, for special administrative uses, information which is not needed on a periodic basis, or which must, to serve its purpose fully, be obtained on a scale which is too time-consuming or costly to warrant collection on a periodic basis. 

One-time reports are requests for information needed at a particular time in relation to a special problem. These are generally requests for information already compiled by the agency.

A summary description of the content and procedural aspects of each of the periodic reports is presented in VI-4000. The report forms and the detailed instructions for their preparation are contained in Part VII, Forms and Procedural Instructions. Further general information concerning special studies appears in VI-5000. Basic materials for particular special studies, i.e., schedules and instructions for their preparation, coding and tabulating instructions, and table forms, are not included because of their temporary character and because the dates when such materials cease to be current are not uniform for State agencies and the 

Bureau. However, States are asked to send promptly two copies of State agency schedules and instructions or plans for collecting and compiling data for voluntary and one-time reports or studies to the appropriate regional office for une information of regional staff. 

H.T. No. 36

I 

1000-5999 

Statistical Reporting and Research Objectives and Methods 

10/23/64 3300.

Principles Governing Reporting Requirements 

A periodic report is required only if it is considered essential to the performance of the Bureau's responsibilities in the administration of the public assistance titles or to efficient administration of 

State public assistance programs.

The introduction of a periodic report on a voluntary basis does not mean that the report will necessarily retain such status permanently. 

In some instances a report may be introduced on a voluntary basis to test the validity of the concepts and definitions that are used, or to determine the administrative usefulness of the information provided, before a decision can be made as to whether it should be required. In other instances, a voluntary report may retain that status permanently, when it is not essential to have the informati on in the report for all States.

The test for required or voluntary periodic reports also applies in substance to special studies. In some instances, studies are required rather than voluntary in order to make it possible to use national samples which provide reliable data on a nationwide basis with a minimum expenditure of effort by State agency staff. 

3400. Interpretation of Information Collected 

In interpreting the information collected, the Bureau draws upon a wide variety of sources of information concerning the organizational, financial, and procedural aspects of State public assistance programs, including editorial reviews for selected periodic reports, administrative reviews conducted by the regional staffs of the Bureau, and reports submitted by them. 

3500. Distribution of Reported Information to State Agencies 

Tabulations and analyses of basic data reported by all State agencies submitting particular reports or special studies are made available to State agencies as fully and promptly as possible. The Bureau seeks to attain this objective through the issuance of: 

1. Periodic releases based on the periodic reports; 

Special publications (e.g., Public Assistance Reports) 

Articles in the Welfare In Review;

and 2. 3. 4. 

Unclassified special releases.

Objectives and Methods Distribution of Reported Information to State Agencies (Continued) 

Each of the periodic releases based on periodic reports is listed in VI-4000, under the summary description of the periodic report from which it is derived. These releases are distributed to State public assistance agencies, and are frequently made available, on request, within the limit of supplies, to other agencies and 

With regard to special publications and unclassified special releases, a small supply of each is usually sent to all State public assistance agencies upon request. In many instances, such publications are also furnished on request, within the limits of supplies, to other agencies and individuals.

Releases and publications will be listed in the annual statistical supplement to the September issue of the Welfare In Review beginning in 1964. 

3600. Consultation to State Agencies 

3700.

Technical consultation is provided to State agencies by the Bureau's regional research analysts on all aspects of the Bureau's statistical reporting and research program and on other matters affecting State programs for statistical reporting and research on which State agencies desire and request such consultation.

State Research Director 

The Public Welfare Amendments of 1962 provided for a new emphasis on services for prevention and rehabilitation. As a consequence, the role of research and statistics, as an integral part of the implementation of these new emphases, calls attention to the need to establish and improve effective research programs. Moreover, States will be developing and participating in demonstration and research programs. Each State public welfare agency must have a minimum of one full-time position to direct the research and statistics program. To give this program adequate attention, States should also establish or strengthen an organizational unit for research and statistics to serve all the programs administered by the State public welfare agency. In those States where there is a separate State agency for the blind, such agency may meet this requirement by a cooperative plan with the State public welfare agency.

Periodic Statistical Reports 

4010. 

Classification of Reports 

In subsequent sections, the periodic reports submitted by State agencies to the Bureau are classified according to frequency of submittal, that is, monthly, quarterly, semiannually, annually, or biennially. A statement at the end of the title of each report indicates whether it is required or voluntary. For each required report the following information is given. (1) Content, (2) Requirements (indicating that a report is required in its entirety or differentiating voluntary items from those that are required), (3) Distribution of Supplies of Forms, 

(4) Submittal Procedure, and (5) Distribution of Reported Information to 

States. For each voluntary report the same information is provided, except for the omission of item (2).

Part IB: 

Total amounts of medical vendor payments for (FS-2078.12) programs under public assistance titles of the 

Social Security Act and for general assistance, by title of the Social Security Act or pro gram under which assistance provided and form of medical vendor payment.

Requirements 

Concurrent receipt of old-age, survivors, and disability insurance by aged recipients of public assistance.

Except for Part IC and specific items in other parts as indicated in the instructions, reporting relating to the federally aided programs is required of all State public assistance or health agencies administering or supervising the administration of approved plans. All items on general assistance are requested on a voluntary basis. 

Conditions for waiver of requirement for reporting county data, Part IV (FS-2078.4) The requirement for reporting county data in Part IV may be waived if county data of the types provided for, or basic data from which such types of data can be derived, are published by the State agency and such published data are made available to the Division of 

Research, Bureau of Family Services, by the last day of the month following the report month. A State wishing to provide published data in lieu of completing Part IV should submit a request for waiver of the reporting requirement to the appropriate regional family services representative. 

Distribution of Supplies of Forms 

Supplies of the constituent forms of this report are distributed to State agencies in June and December for use in the following 6-month periods.

421. Form FS-2069, Semiannual Statistical Report on Social Services Public Assistance (2/65) and Instructions (2/65) - Required 1. Content 

2. 3. 

This report furnishes data on social services provided to current, former and potential recipients of public assistance in States providing such services at the 75 percent rate of Federal financial participation under the Social Security Act Amendments of 1962. The reports cover the first and third quarters of the fiscal year.

The report comprises three parts as follows: 

Part I. 

Includes information on number of defined service cases, classified by problem area, that received social services.

Part II. Includes information on the number of defined service cases that received social services, classified by specified area of service.

Part III Includes information on the number of applicant cases that received social services, classified by specified area of service.

Requirements This report is required for the first and third quarters of the fiscal year of all State public assistance agencies administering or supervising the administration of an approved plan under which services at the 75 percent rate of Federal financial participation are provided under one or more titles of the Social 

Security Act.

Forms PA-2031.1 and PA-2031.2, Semiannual Statistical Reports on Reasons for Opening Public Assistance Cases (5761), and Instruc tions Inst. PA-2031.3 (5/61), Form PA-2031.4 (5/61), and Inst.

PA-2031.5 (5/61) - Voluntary 1. Content 2. 3. 4. The reports on reasons for opening public assistance cases provide information on changes in circumstances of cases that led to their need for and the approval of public assistance. Forms PA-2031.1 

and PA-2031.2 are identical except for the exclusion of inapplicable reasons; Form PA-2031.1 pertains to the adult programs and therefore excludes reasons that are applicable only to the program of aid to dependent children, whereas Form PA-2031.2 pertains to the program of aid to dependent children and therefore excludes reasons that are applicable only to the adult programs.

The reasons for opening classify cases opened into two major groups: (1) those with a material change in income or resources, and (2) those with no material change in income or resources. 

Reasons for opening cases are defined in terms of the occurrence within the 6-month period preceding the application that had the greatest effect in producing the need for assistance for the individual case. Cases opened for each reason are also classified according to whether or not OASDI benefits were being received at the time of approval for assistance.

Distribution of Supplies of Forms 

A supply of the forms is sent to State agencies early in June and December for the semiannual periods ending with June and December. 

Submittal Procedure 

The reports are to be submitted on or before August 15 and 

February 15 for the first and second 6-month periods, respectively, of the calendar year. One copy should be submitted to the Bureau of Public Assistance in Washington, D. C., and one copy should be sent to the appropriate regional office of the Department. 

Distribution of Reported Information to States 

The data in these reports are to be summarized and analyzed in a semiannual release.

4230. Forms PA-2032.1 and PA-2032.2, Semiannual Statistical Reports on Reasons for Closing Public Assistance Cases (5/61), and Instruc tions Inst. PA-2032.3 (5/61), Form PA-2032.4 (5/61), and Inst. 

PA-2032.5 (5/61) - Voluntary 

1. Content 

2. 

The reports on reasons for closing public assistance cases provide information on changes in circumstances of cases that led to the discontinuance of assistance. Forms PA-2032.1 and PA-2032.2 are identical except for the exclusion of inapplicable reasons; Form

PA-2032.1 pertains to the adult programs and therefore excludes reasons that are applicable only to the program of aid to dependent children, whereas Form PA-2032.2 pertains to the program of aid to dependent children and therefore excludes reasons that are applicable only to the adult programs.

The reasons for closing classify cases closed into major groups as follows: (1) those closed because of death; (2) those no longer eligible with respect to need; (3) those no longer meeting eligibility requirements other than need; (4) those transferred to other assistance programs; and (5) those closed for other reasons. Cases closed for each reason are also classified according to whether or not OASDI benefits were being received at the time of closing.

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS I. INTRODUCTION 

The instructions that follow deal with the procedure for submittal of re quired State plan material. The Outline for State Public Assistance 

Plans lists the subject matter which the State plan must cover. The Outline presents in a condensed form the subject matter which is expressly required under the Social Security Act and which will provide a general framework for determining whether a State's proposed plan of operation is likely to result in an administration of public assistance that complies with Federal requirements.

The Outline items identify only the subjects which the approved State plan must cover. The specific content of the plan requirements is to be found in most instances in other parts of the . Reference is therefore made at various points in the Outline to the appropriate sections of the Handbook. As additional Handbook sections are issued or present ones revised, this outline will be amended to reflect the applicability of the Handbook specifications. 

Throughout the document the following initials are used to refer to each of the public assistance programs:

ОАА 

MAA AB for old-age assistance; for medical assistance for the aged; - for aid to the blind;

APTD for aid to the permanently and totally disabled; 

AABD for aid to the aged, blind or disabled, or for such aid and MAA;

AFDC - for aid and services to needy families with dependent children, also referred to as aid to families with dependent children.

The Outline is considered a flexible document and will continue to be subject to necessary revisions for improving its structure on the basis of the States' and Bureau's experience in its use. A. 

Continuity of the Plan 

The State plan represents documentary evidence of current as well as initial conformity with the Social Security Act. The dynamic character of public assistance necessitates frequent modification of the 

H.T. No. 18

1100 Superseded by SRS Program 

Regulation 

10-11, dated 2/27/71. See Federal 

Register, 

Vol. 36, No. 40,

12/20/63 State program in one respect or another in response to changing conditions. The provision in the Act for a quarterly certification of funds is predicated on a finding by the Welfare Administration that the State plan continues to be in conformity, and therefore, the evaluation of the current provisions of the plan at such intervals is necessary. The 

State plan, therefore, at any given time, must represent an accurate picture of the fundamentals upon which current State operations are based. 

Modifications and revisions of the plan are to be submitted currently, Modification or changes in one or more specific aspects of the program when found to be acceptable, will be incorporated in the approved plan and the State will be so notified. When A State has made changes in its plan which alter fundamentally the basis of the State program as to organization, procedure, or standards, a new or substantially rewritten plan is required. A new plan will always be required when the existing plan is changed in a matter of such importance as to amount to a completely new regime or new program, requiring a determination by the Welfare Administration as to the conformity of the new plan with the Social Security Act. In such instances, the previously approved plan will be considered to have been superseded by the new plan. In all other instances, it will be considered that the previously approved plan is of a continuing nature and is merely modified and not superseded. dated 2/27/71, Chapter II 

45 CFR 205.5 B. 

When a State reviɛes it plan substantially without making changes important enough to constitute a new plan, it will be considered a rewritten plan.

In addition to submitting current changes at periodic intervals, the State should review the entire body of its plan, not less than once in three years in order that the State agency may assure the Bureau that all necessary material is in the plan, and that all obsolete material has been removed. This review offers the opportunity to improve the plan as to completeness, consistency, and convenience of form When this reevaluation has been completed, the agency executive should notify the Bureau in writing whether any changes are necessary and if 

So when they may be expected.

Integrated Public Assistance Plans 

In general, plan material to meet a requirement common to all public assistance programs, insofar as they are under the jurisdiction of - 2 - 

H.T. No. 18 

12

1100 

12/20/63 the same State agency, may be submitted in an integrated form covering 

OAA and MAA (title I), AFDC (title IV), AB (title X), and APID (title XIV), or AAED with or without MAA (title XVI), and AFDC (title IV). 

However, when the integrated form is used, it is essential that there be no ambiguity as to the program or programs to which each item of plan material applies. Variations peculiar to each program are to be specified. The State agency is responsible for identifying the provisions that are pertinent to the program(s) under each public assistance title since, legally, a separate plan must be approved as specified in the Act even though the State's materials are submitted in integrated form.

If a State elects to have a MAA program, the State material is an amendment to either the State plan for OAA (title I) or AABD (title 

XVI).

Section 1602(a), last sentence, title XVI permits the States which on January 1, 1962, administered or supervised the administration of the approved plan under title X under a different State agency from that administering or supervising the administration of the approved plans under titles I and XIV, to designate such separate agency to administer or supervise the administration of the portion of the title XVI plan which relates to blind individuals. For these States the plan for blind individuals and the plan for AABD with or without MAA will be regarded as separate plans for purposes of title XVI. 

Another feature peculiar to title XVI is that formal approval of plans submitted under this title will terminate the State's approved plans under titles I, X and XIV, and, thereafter, such States may have only an approved plan under title XVI and an approved plan under title IV. 

As long as AFDC and AAED programs are under the jurisdiction of the same State agency, the plan for title XVI may be integrated with the title IV plan providing the variations peculiar to each program are specified and provisions pertinent to each program are identified. - 3 -

II. USE OF OUTLINE FOR STATE PUBLIC ASSISTANCE PLANS 

The subjects listed in the Outline represent the minimum subjects for which material must be submitted to enable the Bureau to determine the conformity of a State plan, as well as the basis on which the amounts of Federal matching can be determined. The materials required, in general, are the laws, official rules, regulations, and State agency policies governing operation of the program in the State. There is no intention to have the same content repeated under different subjects. Appropriate cross-references to content in the State plan will be satisfactory. For example, a comprehensive public welfare law usually contains both organization and eligibility requirements. In such cases, there needs to be only one submittal of the law. A similar application of this guide should be made throughout the plan wherever pertinent.

Federal financial participation may be made available (provided all other requirements are met) as of the first day of the quarter in which the State submits its original plan in approvable and operative form, regardless of the quarter in which the Commissioner of Welfare takes action. If, however. a State puts its plan into operation prior to the quarter in which its original plan is submitted, Federal participation may not be made available for either assistance or administration for any such prior quarter. 

The Outline in some instances refers to plan requirements that relate to optional features of the public assistance titles, as for example, vendor payments for medical and remedial care and services. Outline items of this kind are, of course, applicable only to State programs that have elected such option. Similarly, the applicability of certain plan requirements will depend on whether the programs are State or locally administered. 

As a matter of general principle, procedural materials pertaining to internal administrative processes need not be submitted as plan material, except when these materials are specified in the Outline or in the cited sections of the  or Handbook Supplement 

Medical Assistance for the Aged as being an integral part of the subject matter governed by a Handbook requirement. When the situation in a particular State requires amplification of certain provisions of its plan in order to clarify possible questions of conformity or matching, specific requests will be made for such amplification of the State plans. In general, detailed operating instructions issued in supplement to the State's basic policies are to be omitted from the plan. For example, the details of a case-numbering procedure or system of filing case-record materials would 

H.T. No. 18 

5-

1100 

12/20/63 not ordinarily be submitted as a part of the State's plan. State agencies, however, are free to submit such procedural material for review if they desire to have the advice of the Bureau. The Bureau has a vital interest in the continued improvement of standards and services in the administration of public assistance, and is anxious to have the State make full use of its advisory and consultative services. The Bureau may have occasion also to request supplementary material to facilitate consultation with the State agencies and for background purposes in developing national standards.

The body of material representing the official content of the approved State plan, at any given time, will be that material which is directly and significantly related to the subjects described in the Outline and identified as such in the transmittal from the State agency. 

A. Form of Submittal 

The form in which plan material is submitted is of primary importance to insure proper identification by the State of the required content of the State plan and to facilitate reference in the review and discussion of plan provisions. It is important also for maintaining a continuing awareness of the formal basis of the State agency relationship with the Welfare Administration, and for preserving continuity of this relationship through transitional periods of change in administration and staffing. The exact form in which the plan should be prepared is not prescribed by the Bureau. Whatever form is adopted by the State for the plan, must be simple, concise, and usable, and so organized that the contents can be readily identified for determining compliance with the Social Security Act. The Bureau recognizes the advantage to State agencies of being able to submit materials in the form prepared for their own use. So far as this is consistent with the above criteria for the form of a plan, such materials may be submitted as plan material.

The use of a comprehensive operating manual, with clear identification of the sections formally submitted in accordance with the Outline, will most acceptably carry out the purposes mentioned in the preceding paragraph. Manuals of operation have been demonstrated by experience to be the most effective means of transmitting agency policies, regulations, standards, and procedures to the staff. In the absence of manuals, there can be little assurance of uniformity of operation throughout the State and efficiency of administration of the public assistance programs. - 6H.T. No. 18 

16

1100 

12/20/63 If the State agency has developed a manual system for use by its staff and the local agencies in the operation of the public assistance program, the manual or set of manuals may be submitted as the State plan, provided the content encompasses the subject matter required in the Outline and is appropriately identified. 

Where no formalized manual system is in operation, or the manual system in use by the State agency is incomplete, it may be possible to cover some of the required elements of the Outline by submitting existing materials. A complete section or volume of a manual system can be submitted as a part of the State plan. A selective submittal of various State agency releases will be accepted only if the transmittal includes specific designation as to the required subject matter which the releases are intended to cover and their appropriate place in the plan. Furthermore, if there is no manual system, it will be necessary for the State agency to prepare material for its plan in an orderly and usable form with an established procedure for keeping the plan current and for proper identification and arrangement. A series of agency bulletins or memoranda without proper identification and specified relationships would not be accepted for purposes of compliance with the Social Security Act. 

B. Submittal of Laws 

New laws must be submitted as soon as possible after enactment, accompanied by a certification from the administrator of the State agency or from the Secretary of State or other authorized official (1) affirming that they are true copies of the laws as enacted; (2) indicating the date of approval or enactment; and (3) giving the effective date of the law.

The transmittal of new laws should also include a statement by the administrator of the agency (1) indicating the laws or sections of laws which each new law amends or renders obsolete, and the category or categories of assistance affected by the new legislation; (2) including a brief description of the changes in the operation of the State plan necessitated by the new legislation; and (3) including a reference identifying the implementing plan material if already submitted, or an indication of when such material will be prepared.

H.T. No. 18 

- 7 -

1100 

12/20/63 The submittal of consolidated public assistance laws covering all or a substantial part of the subject matter listed in the Outline, in the form of a booklet or as part of the State's manual, is especially recommended because of convenience of use and reference. However, the submittal of legal material in this form must be accompanied by a certificate by the administrator of the State agency affirming that the text is a true copy of the laws as enacted. 

Copies of State laws of general applicability related to Section V - Finance of the Outline need not be submitted. To the extent that direct operations of the public assistance programs are affected by these laws, appropriate citations must be included in the State plan material required under Section V- Finance, with respect to: (1) travel procurement, retirement, and other customary items of administrative expense; (2) earmarking and allocation of revenues where public assistance funds come from the general fund; (3) providing for or forbidding transfers among appropriations; and (4) use of emergency or contingency funds. Copies of such laws should be available in the State office for necessary use in audits or other reviews. State personnel laws of general applicability related to

III. TRANSMITTAL PROCEDURE 

Each State agency, responsible for one or more public assistance programs involving Federal participation, will submit six copies of the material that is the State's plan including amendments thereto, except as modified under the optional procedure (see below) for submitting merit system and other common materials required by the Bureau of Family Services and the Children's Bureau. The six copies of the plan material should be mailed to the regional office for the attention of the family services representative except as other transmittal procedure is indicated under the optional procedure. No item of required State plan material is to be submitted without an indication as to its place in the State plan as well as its relationship to other parts of the plan, for such information is pertinent. Where the State agency uses a manual system which includes material pertaining to other welfare programs and procedural matters not included in the Outline of required State plan material, it will be necessary to identify the portions submitted to meet the requirements of titles I, including MAA, IV, X, XIV, and XVI of the Social Security Act. The official submittal of plan material should be over the signature of the executive of the agency. Where authority to make official submittal has been delegated to another position in the agency, such delegation should be a matter of official record in a letter from the executive. 

A. New and Rewritten Plans

A letter of transmittal must accompany the submittal of each new or rewritten plan and should contain: 

1. A list identifying all documents transmitted and, if additional documents are to be submitted to complete the plan, the approximate date upon which each may be expected; volume or section numbers and titles will constitute sufficient identification of compiled documents, provided they are identified individually by a table of contents or index in the volume or section; 2. 

A statement that the documents transmitted, together with any others to be submitted later (and so specified), comprise the State's official plan submitted to the Welfare Administration;

Current Revisions of State Plans 

Revisions of specific aspects of an approved State plan, if of the type required as plan material. will be transmitted by Form FS-553, Submittal and Report of Action on Public Assistance Plan Materials. 

This form is so arranged that it can serve both as an official transmittal by the State agency or plan material, and acknowledgment of receipt and report to the State agency of the action taken by the Bureau. In general, the instructions for the use of this form provide for identification of the subject of the plan material transmitted, the programs affected, the effective date, and the material superseded.

OUTLINE FOR STATE PUBLIC ASSISTANCE PLANS I. LEGAL MATERIALS 

The material required under this section of the Outline includes all constitutional provisions and statutes, with amendments and executive orders, which constitute the legal basis of the public assistance plan. 

The subject areas relating to legal materials to be submitted are for the must part subject areas included in other sections of the Outline. 

It is not intended that there be duplication of legal materials submitted. 

When submitting legal materials a plan-outline reference to "I Legal 

Materials" will be sufficient identification. 

In other sections of the plan, when the Outline specifies legal authorization, it is necessary to cite only the applicable legal material. 

A. Statutes and Constitutional Provisions 

B.

All statutes and constitutional provisions with amendments and executive orders which are directly pertinent to the public assistance programs must be submitted as plan material. These include provisions covering authorization and definition of the scope of the public assistance programs (e.g., eligibility conditions); designation and description of State and local agencies' responsibility for the administration of the public assistance programs (e.g., payment, hearings); definition of the authority and powers of the State and local agencies with respect to rule making, cooperation with the Federal government, selection of personnel, and relationship between State and local agencies, including the proportion of expenditures for assistance and administration paid by State and local governments; and definition of the authority and functions of the State agency with respect to administration of a merit system. 

Judicial Decisions and Opinions of Attorney General 

All judicial decisions and opinions of attorney general relating to public assistance, should be submitted as plan material, where a question of constitutionality of a law has arisen, the intent of the law is not clear, or the authority of the agency to promulgate a particular rule or regulation is in question. - 17 - 

H.T. No. 18

C. Certification by the Attorney General 

For each new p nd whenever there is a major change in the State plan which altus fundamentally the basis of the State program as to organization, a certification by the attorney general must be submitted certifying that the State agency submitting the plan or the revision in the plan (specifying the official title of the agency) is the single State agency under the law of the State vested with authority to administer, or supervise, the public assistance plan or plans, and has authority to adopt rules and regulations governing the administration of the plan. If the plan is to be administered by local political subdivisions, the certification must specify that the plan is mandatory upon them and will be in effect throughout the State, and that the rules and regulations of the State agency are binding upon the political subdivisions, specifying the agency or agencies so bound. III. PERSONNEL ADMINISTRATION 

Materials required under this section of the Outline are necessary for determining conformity with the requirements of the Social Security Act for methods of administration, including those relating to personnel standards on a merit basis, for evaluating compliance with the Standards for a Merit System of Personnel Administration (III-1000-2000; M-2400) 

issued under these requirements, and for a staff development program (III-3000). They also provide information essential to a determination that the State agency has assumed responsibility for merit system or civil service operation in the public assistance programs and for personnel operation within the agency, and for staff development. 

The types of materials outlined under "A" below are essential for compliance of the plans for all State grant-in-aid programs with the merit system standards of the Department of Health, Education, and Welfare. 

The materials under "B" and "C" are specific requirements for public assistance plans in addition to the "A" materials. It is not intended that there be in "B" and "C" any duplication of materials submitted under "A."

However, these subject matter headings should be covered in the State plan, and wherever applicable, a reference only need be made to materials, if any, submitted under "A." Also, there is some material affecting personnel administration which is applicable to Section V Finance of the Outline. The same principle of non-duplication of materials applies to that section of the Outline. 

Staff development in public assistance is covered under "D."

Lavs

Standards for a Merit System of Personnel Administration, issued initially on September 1, 1948 and as amended in 

January 1963 (State Letter No. 625) (III-2000).

Classification and Compensation Plans 

Specifications and salary ranges for all classes of positions in the State public assistance agency, or State and local public assistance agencies, engaged in the administration of the State public assistance plan(s). These are: 8. 

State department - Class specifications and salary ranges for administrative, professional, technical, and clerical positions engaged in administrative direction and supervision of the program and its operation, and performance of facilitating staff services.

Where local agencies responsible for public assistance programs are served by local civil services systems, the State agency is to adopt and submit the regulations or standards that the agency will use in reviewing and approving the local civil service provisions, as applied in the public assistance programs, in all the counties in which such systems are in effect for such local agencies. However, if only one local agency responsible for public assistance programs in the State is served by a local civil service system, the State agency may instead elect to submit as a part of the plan, copies of the local civil service ordinances, rules and regulations, class specifications and salary ranges, provided the State agency, responsible for public assistance programs, identifies in the material submitted the provisions that differ from the merit system applicable to other such local agencies. States having more than one local agency responsible for public assistance programs served by local service systems, are not to submit locally adopted civil service provisions. 24H.T. No. 18

D. Agency's Responsibility for a Staff Development Program 

(III-3000) This part of the Outline is intended to give emphasis to strengthening staff development programs and to the State agency's responsibility for organizing a continuous program of staff development, as an essential function in its personnel administration. Plan material, required under this part, will serve as a basis of determining conformity of the State plan under titles I, IV, X, XIV and XVI of the Social Security Act with the requirement of each title, regarding methods of administration for the proper and efficient operation of the plan, and will furnish information necessary to determine the extent of Federal financial participation. 

The general principle concerning procedural materials which pertain to internal administrative processes is applicable here; i.e., that such materials are not to be submitted as plan material, except when specified in the Outline or in other cited sections of the Handbook as being an integral part of the subject matter. For example, in describing the agency's in-service training programs, detailed materials, such as copies of teaching outline or guides to supervisors, would not be submitted as a part of the State's plan. It is not necessary to repeat plan material; wherever applicable only a cross reference need be made. There is some material affecting staff development programs applicable to two sections of this - 27

IV. ELIGIBILITY, ASSISTANCE, AND SERVICES 1/ 

Plan materials required under this section are those necessary for determining conformity with the Social Security Act--(a) the required and prohibited eligibility conditions specified in the Act; (b) the requirements on proper and efficient administration in the Act; (c) the definitions of assistance to or in behalf of individuals in the Act; (a) the provision of services in support of the purposes of strengthening family life, self-support and self-care set forth in the Act; (e) and other requirements in the Act relating to agency responsibility in dealing with applicants for and recipients of assistance. In addition, the plan materials will furnish information necessary to determine the extent of Federal financial participation. 

Each State plan must set forth all the requirements relating to eligibility as defined by the Act and any State-imposed conditions that affect the scope of the Federal-State plan. When State programs permit inclusion of groups other than those encompassed by the Act, the State plan must provide for limiting requests for Federal financial participation to or for recipients who are within the definitions of the Act.

F. Social Services in Public Assistance (IV-4000) 

State plan material is required under this subject heading as a basis for claiming any Federal financial participation in costs of providing social services. The required plan material in this area is different in that there are two rates of Federal financial participation involved with mandatory and optional plan requirements. In addition, there is the feature of progressive implementation of certain plan requirements which must be fully in effect by July 1, 1967. In view of these differences, it is essential that plan provisions implementing the requirements in IV-4000 cover each of the optional plan requirements. For those which the 

State agency does not elect to implement, the plan must include an affirmative statement to this effect.

SUPPLEMENT TO SECTION IV 

Medical Assistance for the Aged

Medical Assistance for the Aged (MAA) is covered separately in this section of the Outline because of the number of differences between this program under titles I and XVI and the other programs under these titles. 

These differences include certain eligibility provisions, scope of medical and remedial services, and some special program requirements. Also, payments are not made in the form of money payments to the individual, but all are vendor payments in behalf of the elderly person needing medical care. Because of these several differences, it is feasible to cover 

MAA in a supplement to section IV. The overall statements in the introductory paragraphs in section IV of the Outline are applicable to MAA. 

Election of MAA part of titles I and XVI as a specified service for 75 percent rate of Federal participation is permissible only when State's approved plan provides for prescribed services under OAA (title I) or under AABD (title XVI).

1. 2. 3.

M-3230, item 4; IV-2331; IV-A-2331, paragraph 2) 

4. Rule providing for content and maintenance of case records supporting initial and continuing eligibility (IV-2232) 5. Legal base and administrative procedures for dealing with facts that present suspicion of fraud by applicants for or recipients of medical assistance for the aged.

V. FINANCE 

Materials required under this section are necessary for determining that the methods of financing meet the requirements of the Social Security Act by providing for financial participation from State funds in total expenditures and assuring that the plan will be in effect in all political subdivisions of the State; that there are proper fiscal and accounting controls for all funds received or disbursed under the responsibility of the State and local public assistance agencies, and adequate methods of assuring the correctness of reports on which Federal grants are based; and that the State agency has established standards and regulations governing the expenditures necessary for the proper and efficient administration of the plan.

The bases for the materials required for this section of the plan are the provisions of State laws, agency policy, rules and regulations, and fiscal regulations of other State or local departments which govern the types of expenditures authorized for State and local administration. 

State laws of general applicability, other than personal service laws, need not be submitted. However, when an agency has invoked any of these laws in the direct operations of its programs, such laws or appropriate parts should be cited with a statement identifying their content and showing how they apply to public assistance expenditures. It is not intended that there be detailed instructions, nor that forms be submitted unless the agency considers it necessary or that it is a more concise way of covering the subject. However, the data should be available to the regional office for use in connection with the Federal audit of review of expenditure reports.
