QUALITY CONTROL OF CASE ACTIONS IN PUBLIC ASSISTANCE(QC) GUIDES AND INSTRUCTIONS FOR STATE AGENCY USE 

(See State Letter No.1089 and 1090, dated 10/19/70; and Manual 

"QC Quality Control in Public Assistance, July 1970.") 

1.

VII 

Budget Bureau No. 122-R064.1 

(6/65) 

DEFARTMENT OF HEALTH, EDUCATION, AND WELFARE Welfare Administration Bureau of Family Services QUALITY CONTROL OF CASE ACTIONS IN PUBLIC ASSISTANCE (QC) I. A GENERAL STATEMENT OF THE OBJECTIVES, METHODS AND SCOPE OF QUALITY CONTROL 

The system of quality control of case actions in public assistance is a required method of administration whose basic objective is to provide a systematic and continuous control by the State agency over the quality and accuracy of case actions taken by the local public assistance agencies. It is carried out in accordance with methods and processes established for nationwide use by the Bureau of Family Services.

In addition to providing a State tool for assuring control of the quality of local performance in the program areas it covers, QC activities also yields reliable information on whether applicants and recipients receive the assistance for which they are eligible and whether assistance funds go only to eligible persons in correct amounts.

As a method of State administration, QC encompasses four general processes: (1) a continuous review of samples of local agency case actions throughout the State; (2) assembly and analysis of case action findings; (3) the planning and carrying out of corrective measures to deal with significant problem areas as they come to the State agency's attention; and (4) periodic assembly and reporting to the Bureau of findings and results. 

The QC system is intended to support and contribute to the States' efforts toward program development, strengthening of services to applicants and recipients, and more efficient State supervision of local agencies. It provides a means of testing the effectiveness and clarity of State policy and procedures, of contributing to strengthened State and local staff training and supervision, and it can serve to demonstrate a high standard for field investigation.

As an instrument for corrective and remedial action, the focus of QC is on locating the types of errors frequently occuring in the program, determining why they occur, and bringing on the action by which they can be brought to a minimum level of incidence. It is thus concerned with individual case errors that are found as indications of problem areas in overall agency performance that need to be given remedial treatment. 

From information furnished by the reviewer, the State may determine that Federal matching has been improperly claimed. It is expected that proper adjustment of Federal funds will be made in accordance with Handbook 

IV-5514. 

(6/65) 

H.T. No. 59 79

I }) ))

A GENERAL STATEMENT 

(cont.)

The extent to which QC contributes to these objectives will be dependent, in part on the skill with which reviewers conduct investigations, the competency with which they evaluate local agency performance, and the care with which they record their findings. In addition, the accomplishment of its remedial objectives requires the close working partnership between all of the various elements of agency administration involved and sound integration of total agency knowledge and skills in the QC process in order to make the system and its results fully meaningful.

In the review of local agency actions, QC evaluates local agency decisions on eligibility and amount of payment, and the methods and processes used in reaching these decisions. The sample of actions reviewed includes "positive" and "negative" actions--a positive action being an approved application or a redetermination of eligibility, and a negative action being a rejected application or a discontinuance of assistance. Through continuous statistical sampling of these case actions and review of the sample case actions drawn, the State agency is enabled to estimate the quality of work in the entire group of actions from which the sample was taken. Because local practice is under continuous review in the QC process, it is also possible to estimate the effectiveness of the measures taken to correct defects previously noted, and at the same time, to identify the emergence of any new problems as they arise.

Reviews of case actions are conducted through case record analysis and field investigations. A review schedule is completed for each case to determine whether or not the case decision, and the steps leading to that decision, were carried out in accordance with State agency policy. 

As case action reviews are completed, the findings are assembled in central (State) office for continuous analysis and tabulation. When, in this process, the number of defective case actions reaches a pre-determined level--the "action level"--certain additional steps are automatically triggered: (1) an analysis of the case action findings to determine the nature of the problems that exist, how they are caused, and to define the measures necessary to eliminate or reduce these problems; and (2) development and implementation of the steps determined to be necessary to eliminate or reduce the problems.

Scope of QC 

The QC review covers local agency actions relating to eligibility for money payment in two groups: 1. 

Positive actions (applications approved for payment and redeterminations of eligibility which resulted in continuation of assistance) (6/65) 

H.T. No. 59 

))

I 

2. Negative actions (applications denied, withdrawn, or otherwise disposed of without payment, and terminations of assistance).

Not included in the system are (1) AFDC-Foster Care cases; (2) cases involving vendor medical care payments only (including MAA); (3) denied applications and discontinuances resulting from the death of the recipient; and (4) the denial and discontinuance portion of transfers between categories covered by the QC system. 

The review of the elements of social study is presently included in the scope of the QC system on an optional basis to States at this time. 

Ultimately, a broader area of social services will be brought within the scope of QC as a required part of the system. Also to be added to the system are cases involving vendor medical care payments only. 

A number of related facets of local agency performance with respect to cases are not at present included in the QC system. These related requirements include notification to law enforcement officials, promptness of action on applications, promptness of action on closings and notification to the client of the agency's decision. This does not mean that they are of lesser significance, and some means of review of local agency compliance with these and other similar requirements should likewise be a part of State supervision or administration. If agencies wish to combine review of these subjects with the QC review, this is permissible. (Rev. 6/66) 

H.T. No. 82

TT 

QUALITY CONTROL REVIEW 

The review of case actions is the first phase of the QC system in which the QC reviewer plays the major role. As sample case actions are drawn, in accordance with the State's sampling processes, they are assigned for review. The QC review encompasses analysis of the case record, the conduct of a field investigation, and an evaluation of the correctness of the action taken by the local agency.

Quality control is applied not to a sample of cases, but to a sample of local agency actions on cases. Further, the review focus is on the correctness of the local agency's action at the time it was taken, in the light of the situation as it existed at that time. It is therefore important that reviews be made as soon after the local agency's action as possible, consistent with sampling techniques and administrative feasibility. As the length of time following the case action increases, the review process becomes more difficult because the situation as it was known to the local agency cannot be duplicated. 

The QC review includes examination of the quality of each of the steps taken by the local agency in the process of reaching a decision on eligibility and amount of payment, the correctness of the local agency's application of State policy to the facts it obtains, and the correctness of the final decision to provide or deny assistance. This scope of review applies equally to positive and negative case action reviews. 

The review is carried out in accordance with the State agency's instructions on information to be obtained, methods of obtaining it, and the policies to be applied in making decisions on eligibility and amount of payment. A.

Methods and Tools 

То Case record analysis and field investigation constitute the review. 

The QC reviewer is expected to bring to his task a knowledge of the program requirements of his State, interviewing skill, and a capacity for analytical judgment. His chief tools are the schedules, Form 

FS-341 for review of positive case actions, and Form FS-342 for review of negative case actions. These forms provide a systematic means for helping the reviewer to analyze the case record, plan and carry out the field investigation, and record findings and evaluation. assist in this purpose, Form FS-341 has reduced the total determination process to its components, called "Elements of Eligibility Determination."

This is intended to simplify the review process by providing a place for the reviewer to record his observations and evaluation in relation to each step that the local agency was expected to go through in reaching its determination. It will be necessary for most States to add to the list of elements to encompass all of the provisions of their plans. 

It is important that reviewers see the QC review as a dynamic analysis of case situations. The use of code numbers in the schedules to indicate evaluation does not mean that this is reduced to a bare statistic. 

The chief purpose of recording evaluations in coded form is to (Rev. 6/66)

H.T. No. 82

II )) 

A. Methods and Tools 

B. C. 

(cont.) facilitate tabulation which will enable the State agency to readily identify those elements most subject to defect by the local agencies in carrying out the program. The written comments of the reviewers on these schedules showing the specific nature of the defect and how it was detected, are equally important in providing leads as to the causes of defects for use in planning corrective action. 

Reviewers will need to supplement these schedules with other forms in some instances. The State's own budget form should be used to compute a budget, if this is indicated, to substantiate the amount of payment in a case. The State's forms for clearance, appointments, inter-office communications, and the like, should also be used as appropriate. 

Relationships With Local Agency 

It is not necessary that the QC review of case actions be conducted in isolation from the local agency. However, the case actions to be reviewed must be selected without any advance notice to the local agency to avoid bias in the sample. The case record is the reviewer's source of information about the agency's action, and it is not appropriate for a reviewer to question a caseworker about what he had done, or to accept oral information not in the record. If current dictation is not transcribed in the case record at the time of the review, arrangements should be made for the recording to be brought up to date. 

It is expected that the local agency will be promptly notified about QC review findings in individual cases. The reviewer's findings and conclusions in regard to case actions, eligibility, and payment are considered final decisions insofar as quality control is concerned, and interchanges subsequent to the QC decision are considered to fall within usual supervisory activities, rather than QC review.

Review of Positive Case Actions 

State requirements, as set forth in manuals or other instructional materials, provide the base for determining whether methods used and the facts obtained around any eligibility or payment element were proper. Each applicable element of eligibility determination must be reviewed to the point mere the reviewer is able to reach a definitive conclusion as to correctness or incorrectness of the local agency's decision. The term "definitive conclusion" is to be interpreted to mean definitive within the framework of State instructions given to the local agencies. The reviewer is expected to use the same methods of investigation as would an experienced, alert, imaginative local agency case worker. The reviewer may make use of any established agency facilities for determining and substantiating (Rev. 6/66)

H.T. No. 82

II C. 

Review of Positive Case Acticns 

(cont.) facts, such as special resource units. The QC staff may also arrange to make use of local agency caseworker staff in certain circumstances to obtain facts. The reviewer will not be expected to follow prescribed methods which necessitate a prolonged casework relationship or use of certain specified methods discussed in item 2. Field Investigation.

With respect to exploration of potential resources or sources of income, the local agency defect would lie in the failure to explore the potential, irrespective of whether or not it may subsequently become an actual resource of income. 

The reviewer must consider whether the local agency followed a Stateprescribed method of obtaining the facts necessary to establish each element of eligibility. Failure to follow the prescribed method is a defect, irrespective of whether the method used produced correct results. For example, if a State specifies that age must be established by a copy of the original birth certificate only, no other proof would be acceptable. For elements for which the State does not prescribe specific methods for substantiating the facts, the reviewer will need to consider the adequacy of the methods used by the local agency in terms of the results they produced. 

1. Case record review.--The analysis of the case record made by the reviewer will be for the purpose, first, of identifying those elements of eligibility which have already been properly established by documentary evidence and are not subject to change. (e.g., age, which the record shows has been previously verified through inspection of the birth certificate). These elements will not need to be reviewed beyond the point of case record analysis, and an evaluation of the appropriateness of agency method and decision with regard to this element can be made at this point.

The case record analysis will, secondly, provide a means of identifying those elements of eligibility determination which must be followed-up by field investigation before an evaluation of the correctness of the agency's action can be made. These include (a) elements established by client's statement, caseworkers observation, or without documentation and (b) any other element that the reviewer may have questions about. With respect to factors which, may be subject to change, the reviewer will need to consider the recency of substantiating evidence in relation to the specific situation and the likelihood of change. (6/65) 

H.T. No. 59

(C

II C. 

Review of Positive Case Actions 

(cont.) }) 

2. Field investigation.--Field investigation is required for all cases included in the sample of positive case actions. It includes interviews with the client and any other persons required by the State plan to be contacted by the local agency worker, collateral correspondence, review of documents, telephone conversations, computations made by the reviewer, and/or any other activity which is pertinent to the review of the case action. An occasional exception must be made to the requirement for field investigation of all positive case actions. Investigation would not be possible when, subsequent to the local agency's action but prior to the time of the review, the client died or moved beyond the jurisdiction of the local agency. Temporary absence of the client would not obviate the need for review provided assistance was still continuing, and it would be necessary for the reviewer to hold the case until a contact with the client could be made. Temporary absence is interpreted to mean a period of time not to exceed three (3) months, provided the review can be completed in time to be included in the report for which the sample is drawn. If it is known, at the time the review is initiated that the client will be absent for a period of three months, or until after the time when all reviews must be completed for the current reporting period, the case should be immediately dropped from the review. 

If in the course of a review, a client is found to be ineligible, the review is nevertheless to continue until all elements of eligibility determination have been reviewed, if this is at all possible, since the primary purpose of QC is to locate all defects in establishing eligibility.

The fact that assistance may have been discontinued prior to the review of a positive case action does not obviate the need for review of the validity of that action. It might be, however, that the client, since he is no longer a recipient, would refuse to cooperate in the review, which would render it impossible. 

When a field investigation is not possible, due to circumstances beyond the agency's control, for reasons given above, the case review will be classified as "Not reviewed".

The following factors must be taken into consideration in completing field investigation activities:

Home visit: 

The major field activities of the QC reviewer in all instances will be to interview in the home and secure information from the recipient (and members of the recipient group insofar as necessary). Appointments in relation to the home visit may be made if provided for in the State QC review system. (6/65) 

H.T. No. 59

11 C. 

Review of Positive Case Actions (cont.) 

2. Field investigation (cont.) b. 

Information obtainable from the local agency: The State agency QC policies should specify the extent to which the following methods are adopted. If the reviewer determines that additional factual Information is necessary, it is proper to request the local agency to obtain this information unless contraindicated by the instructions given below. 

The intent of using local agency participation is to expedite the obtaining of information necessary to the QC review, without relieving the reviewer of his responsibility for evaluating the information and reaching an objective and factually supported conclusion regarding the local agency's action. 

Information secured by the local agency other than that obtained by a resource unit or resource worker will be acceptable only if it is provided in documented form from its source: Examples include wage reports by employers, school attendance, verification by school officials, and property verification by an appropriate agency.

If the reviewer, upon completion of the home visits, finds additional information necessary to reach a conclusion regarding the local agency's action, the local agency should be requested to secure factual information needed by the reviewer, in accordance with the general arrangements which will need to be made in advance with local agencies. This method will apply when the facts necessary are to be secured from or through a person(s) or agency other than the recipient (or other members of the assistance group). 

Local agency assistance may also be requested to obtain needed information when it must be secured from some process conducted in the locality. An example is the need for certain medical information, in which case the reviewer would request the local agency to refer the recipient for a physical examination and to later submit the medical report. 

The role of the local agency, under this method would be limited to securing, upon request, specifically identified factual information, which will not in any way involve the local agency in making judgments or reaching conclusions about the eligibility of the family. The responsibility for evaluating the information and making a decision regarding the case action being reviewed, remains solely that of the QC reviewer. (6/65) 

H.T. No. 59

II 

C. Review of Positive Case Actions 

2. Meld investigation 

(cont.) (cont.) 

The reviewer should not request the local agency to secure information, where it would be simpler and more convenient for him to secure it himself, e.g., rental needs to be verified with landlord, who lives next door to recipient. 

On visiting the recipient, the reviewer can at the same time verify rental with the landlord if this is a plan. requirement.

The reviewer also will retain responsibility for obtaining information from a third party, whenever a contradiction is noted between the information secured from the record and that obtained from the recipient (except as outlined in c below), and where the contradiction cannot be resolved by consulting or obtaining a public document or similarly reliable source of verification. Here the reviewer should go directly to the third party (or source) so that the information necessary to resolve the discrepancy can be carefully weighed. 

Flements not to be pursued in the field investigation:

Certain State plan provisions warrant special consideration in making QC field investigation and in reaching a "definitive conclusion." First, it is not intended that the QC system involve the reviewer in a casework relationship other than that resulting from a short-term contact. The reviewer should not attempt to complete, to conclusion, an element of eligibility which under State plan requires the establishing of a more extended casework relationship. Thus, the reviewer should not undertake to carry out required State methods of the following types: clearances with relatives about their willingness to contribute, steps leading to working out a child care plan in relation to employability of a mother, an unemployed father's willingness to accept training away from home or hazardous working conditions, procedures involving initiation of court referrals, procedures relating to a recipient's accepting or rejecting medical treatment, and considerations governing need for foster home care. example, the investigation would not extend to the question of an absent parent's willingness to begin contributions, but the location of an absent parent and the determination of whether he is making a contribution are properly a part of the QC investigation.

For (6/65) 

H.T. No. 59

II 

C. Review of Positive Case Actions 

(cont.) 

2. Field investigation (cont.) 3. 

It will be necessary for the QC director to identify and list all such methods as exceptions to the field investigation review process for the guidance of all QC reviewers.

When the local case action under review includes questions relating to such an identified exception, the reviewer does not follow the prescribed State plan steps in order to complete the QC review. Under these circumstances when the reviewer finds that the local agency did not undertake or follow the prescribed procedures, the reviewer reaches a "definitive conclusion" with the finding that a defect in method or process has occurred but that a defect as to eligibility or payment is not involved.

Evaluation of case action.--During the process of case analysis and field investigation, and also upon its completion, the reviewer determines the correctness or incorrectness of agency action relative to the various applicable elements of eligibility determination. This evaluation will be made in terms of the last evidence available to the local agency at the time its action was taken. Thus, if the local agency took proper action on the most recent wage information available to it there would be no question raised if the reviewer found a subsequent change in wages. 

4. Review of the elements of social study. --The review of the elements of social study will be optional with States. States wishing to review these elements may utilize Supplement A 

(AFDC) and Supplement B (Adult) of Form FS-341. The review of the elements of social study is obviously limited by the nature of the reviewer's role which is not that of the caseworker. The 

QC reviewer should be alert to evidence of problems or needs which are readily observable or which come to light in the course of his exploration of the client's eligibility. The reviewer would not be expected to consult collateral sources about the existence of problems.

Likewise, the reviewer's evaluation of the plan for service and how it is being carried out would necessarily be subject to the limitations in this situation. (6/65)

VII-1200 

H.T. No. 59 DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH, EDUCATION, AND WELFARE Welfare Administration Bureau of Family Services QUALITY CONTROL OF POSITIVE CASE ACTIONS INSTRUCTIONS FOR REVIEW SCHEDULE FORM FS-341 (Rev.) INSTRUCTION 

Form FS-341 is to be prepared for each money payment case in which the action to be reviewed is approval of an application or redetermination of eligibility in the categories of AFDC, QAA, AB, APTD, or AABD (Title XVI. 

The schedule, consisting of two main divisions and six (6) parts, offers a structured way of examining the validity of local agency determinations by considering the quality and accuracy of activity on the several steps in the process. Specifically the schedule provides for: (1) recording some basic facts about the case and the household; (2) serving as an analytical tool for evaluation of local agency actions, as shown in the case record, with respect to various specified elements; (3) indicating activity of the QC reviewer which produced confirming or conflicting findings; and (4) organizing so that weaknesses in performance can be identified and reported by their nature, frequency of occurrence, local agency involved, and their impact on the correctness of the agency's over-all determination of eligibility and amount of payment according to State plan. 

Schedule Proper -- consists of Parts I through IV. These parts encompass identifying information, evaluation of the elements of eligibility determination, findings on eligibility and amount of payment, and analysis of defects found. The Schedule Proper is to reflect the end product of the reviewer's evaluation; the Work Sheets (see below) are to reflect the detailed basis for this evaluation. 

Schedule Work Sheets consists of Parts V and VI. These parts encompass the face sheet and the individual elements of eligibility determination to be evaluated with space for entering appropriate remarks in relation to the case record review and field investigation. These work sheets are principally intended for use by the reviewer in gathering, comparing, and analyzing data for the completion of the Schedule 

Proper. They may also serve as a supervisory tool.

The QC reviewer will need to make judgments on the adequacy and suitability of the methods the local agency has used, and record on the Schedule Work Sheets the basis of his judgment and the steps he has taken in his field investigation On the Schedule Proper, he will code these findings and analyze the defects found. Uniform understanding 05

11 1 

(cont.) of the purpose and use of the form is essential to insure maximum usefulness of findings.

The provisions of the State plan will be the source of instructions as to the specific elements of eligibility determination which are germane, the manner in which they are to be applied, and the methods by which they are to be established. 

I. IDENTIFYING INFORMATION 

General: This part of the schedule provides for certain basic facts about the case. Some of these data, such as lot and review number will be tabulated for statistical purposes. Clerical personnel may enter some of the information for the benefit of the reviewer. 1. 2. 

Case Name and Address: Self-explanatory. 

3. Category: Here the reviewer will indicate the type of assistance the recipient is receiving. In the combined categories of AABD (Title XVI), the reviewer will indicate whether the recipient's eligibility is based on age, blindness, or disability. 

4. Date of Action: Enter the date, as identified by the local agency, on which the action to be reviewed was taken.

5. Date Assigned: 

Enter the date the case was assigned to the reviewer. 

6. Date Completed: Here the reviewer enters the date when he has completed his investigation, has made his evaluation of local agency performance, and is ready to submit the schedule to the supervisor or central office. 

7. Case Number: Self-explanatory. 

8. 

Caseworker: (Completion of this item is optional.) If it is the 

State practice, enter the name of the local agency worker who conducted the investigation which resulted in the action under review. 

9. Reviewer: Enter the name of the person making the review. 

10. Agency: Enter the name of the local agency from which case was selected. (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59

II 

(cont.) 

11. Type of Action: Circle the appropriate number to indicate whether this schedule is to be used for an approval (new or reopened case) or a redetermination. Approval means the agency's decision to grant assistance in a specified amount to an applicant on the basis of facts developed during the investigation following the application. Redetermination means (1) a reexamination by the agency of factors of eligibility subject to change following a period of time during which the recipient has been receiving assistance and (2) a decision as to continuing eligibility and payment. A case reported as a redetermination in which some but not all elements of eligi. bility were reinvestigated would properly be listed as a positiv! case action if a reaffirmation of the total eligibility of the client had been made by the local agency. In this event, the redetermination would, of course, be classified as defective with respect to elements not properly redetermined. On the other hand, if some but not all elements of eligibility were reinvestigated in a case listed as a redetermination in which it was clear the local agency did not consider that a full redetermination had been made, it should not have been listed for sampling. 12.

Lot and Review Number: Enter the number of the lot from which this sample case action was drawn and the serial number assigned to this case in the lot. This information will be supplied to the reviewer by the State office. These numbers should be hyphenated; e.g., "1001" would be lot 1 case action number 1. 

13. Amount of Payment: Enter the amount of the monthly payment which the recipient received, or was authorized to receive, at the time of the local agency action being reviewed. General:

II. EVALUATION OF ELEMENTS OF ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION 

The purpose of Part II is to reflect in a concise manner the final decision of the reviewer as to the quality and accuracy of the local agency in the process of determining eligibility.

Section A: Flements of Eligibility Determination 

The elements of eligibility are listed in five (5) main groupings called program areas, numbered 10, 20, 30, 40, and 50. The reviewer will record, by a check mark (√) under one of the three codes 0, 1, or 9, his evaluation of the adequacy of each step in the process of determining eligibility. The findings of the case record review and field investigation as reflected on the Work Sheets, will be the basis for this evaluation. After coding all elements in a given program area, the (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59

II )) 

 (cont.) 

reviewer will then code the program area as a whole by inserting either o (no defect) or code 1 (defective action) in the box provided in the X column. A defect in any of the elements in a given program area results in the program area box (column X) being coded defective. In program area 50 Other, space has been provided for States to add addi tional individual State requirements. (Note Part VI.) 

Code -- No defect 

This code is checked when the field investigation fully confirms the local agency's action, as recorded in the case record, in properly obtaining and substantiating facts and reaching proper decisions or conclusions on them. If code 0 is checked, no other code may be considered for this element.

Code 1 

Defective action 

This code is checked for any element with respect to which the local agency (a) failed to follow the methods prescribed in the State manual as required for obtaining and/or verifying relevant facts, (b) failed to apply State policy correctly to the facts at hand in reaching a conclusion in accordance with State plan, or (c) where an element was found to be defective despite the fact that the local agency used prescribed or adequate methods and applied policy correctly to the facts at hand, e.g., situations in which true facts were not obtained by the local agency due to inaccurate information obtained from a supposedly reliable source or concealment by the client of facts not otherwise apparent.

Failure to follow State-prescribed methods ("a" above) is a defect whether or not the use of the correct prescribed method would have resulted in any different information from that actually used by the local agency.

In the absence of a State specified method for obtaining and/or verifying the facts regarding an element, this code is checked if, in the reviewer's judgment, the methods used by the local agency were inadequate to produce and/or substantiate necessary, reliable information.

It would be expected that an incorrect application of State policy ("b" above) to correct facts would generally result in an error either in the amount of payment or in the eligibility status of some or all of the recipients in the case. Included under this heading are misinterpretation of policy, non-compliance with policy, errors in computation of requirements or income, and errors or omissions in the use of standards of assistance. If code 1 is checked, no other code may be considered for this element.

Code 9 

Not applicable -· This code is to be checked for any element which is not applicable in the program area 10 Basic Program Requirements; also, where appropriate, for element 35 "Treatment of income" (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59 

---

II 

(cont.) In in program area 30 Need - Income, and for program area 50 Other. all other instances, each element is deemed to be applicable to every case in that the local agency must consider, in every instance, whether or not it exists with respect to the case; hence, code 9 cannot be used. 

If code 9 is checked, no other code may be used for the element. Code X This columm is to be completed after the elements for the individual program areas have been evaluated and coded. The boxes opposite the different program areas, e.g., 10, 20, etc., are to be coded by inserting the figure 0 or 1 depending on whether none of the elements were found to be defective or whether one or more of the elements encompassed by a program area were found to be defective. Code 9 is never used in this column.

Section B: 

Evaluation Summary 

When the QC review is completed, circle the appropriate code to indicate whether or not any defect(s) in the local agency's action was found. Code 1, "One or more elements defective", will be circled if any of the elements of eligibility determination in Part II-A were checked as being code 1. Otherwise, circle code 0, "No defect found".

If for some reason a case properly selected in the sample was not reviewed, circle code 2 "Not reviewed". This might occur, for example, when review is impossible because the client has died or moved beyond the jurisdiction of the agency subsequent to the date of the local agency's action but before the review could be undertaken. It is to be noted, however, that a case is still within the local agency's jurisdiction if the recipient lives elsewhere in the State but continues to be a recipient of that local agency. In such cases, a field investigation would be required. Another example of a "not reviewed" case would be those instances in which field investigations have been initiated but cannot be completed for some reason. When this results in inability to complete Part III of the Schedule Proper, the total case should be classified as "not reviewed". The reason for not reviewing must be indicated in the space provided. If this item is checked, this page of the schedule must show complete identification of the case, but no entries need be made on the remainder of the schedule.

III. FINDINGS ON ELIGIBILITY AND AMOUNT OF PAYMENT 

General: This part provides for reporting the status of the total case with respect to eligibility and correctness of payment. It is not a summary of the information on the preceding page of the schedule, but additional information which does not appear elsewhere. (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59 

69

II 

(cont.) 

Since C is a review of local agency determinations, these findings are to be made as of the date when the local agency's determination was completed. Any subsequent change which might be found by the reviewer to have taken place is not to be indicated on this schedule. The new information regarding eligibility or amount of payment should, however, be conveyed to the local agency in writing with a copy attached to the schedule.

A. Eligibility According to State Plan 

In this section the reviewer circles the code for the item which describes the eligibility status of the case at the time of the local agency's determination.

All persons eligible. This item means that the field investigation has established that all persons found eligible by the local agency to receive assistance under this category were in fact eligible for such assistance. (Errors in the amount of assistance would be indicated in Section C.) 

Some but not all persons ineligible. This item applies to cases in which the field investigation has established that one or more persons found eligible by the local agency to receive assistance under this category were not in fact eligible under this category, but the total case was not ineligible. As used in the context of WC, "eligible" refers to eligibility under the category as defined by the State, and does not necessarily correspond to persons identified as eligible recipients for Federal matching. 

Cases in which some but not all persons were found to be ineligible are to be coded according to the effect on the amount of payment. 

Resulting in incorrect payment is to be coded where the inclusion of a person ineligible under the State's definition of eligibility resulted in the payment of an amount other than that to which the case should have been entitled under the category. The fact that an ineligible person might have been eligible for payment under some other category does not alter the fact that an incorrect payment has been made under the category reviewed.

No effect on amount of payment is to be coded where the inclusion of an ineligible person made no difference in the amount of assistance paid to the case under the category being reviewed. 

Total case ineligible. This item applies to cases in which the field 

Investigation has established that no person was eligible for assistance under the category for which the case was certified by the local agency. (For cases found to be totally ineligible, no entry regarding the amount of payment is to be made in Section C.). (Rev. 6/65) 

H.T. No. 59 })

B.

Element(s) Directly Involved in Incorrect 

Determination of Eligibility 

For each case coded 2, 3, or 4, in Section A, the reviewer is to indicate on the appropriate line the specific element or elements of eligibility which directly resulted in the finding of ineligibility for some or all persons in the case. The elements are to be identified in the same terms and with the same number as appear on page 1 of the Schedule Proper. c.

Amount of Payment According to State Plan 

This section is applicable only to those cases in which some or all recipients were found through field investigation to have been eligible at the time of the local agency's determination of eligibility. The reviewer is to circle the code which identified whether the payment made or authorized by the local agency at the time of its last determination was found through field investigation to be correct, or over or under the amount to which the recipient was entitled at that time. For cases coded as totally ineligible in Section A, Section C is not applicable, and should be stricken from the schedule by a diagonal line. 

For cases involving overpayment or underpayment, the amount by which the payment made or authorized as a result of the action under review exceeds or falls short of the amount to which the client was properly entitled is to be entered in the space provided. Regardless of the agency's plan for making payments, the amount of over- or underpayment for the period of a month is to be entered on the form. 

IV. ANALYSIS OF DEFECTS IN CASE ACTION 

General: The chief purpose of the entries in Part IV is to provide information on the nature of the defects which may be useful to State office in providing leads as to the causes of defects and undertaking corrective action.

Elements: In this column, enter the number(s) of the elements of eligibility determination that were coded as defective (code 1) in Part II-A. 

Nature of Defect: Opposite the individual defective elements in column 1, the QC reviewer will show the specific nature of all discrepancies or errors noted. The information for this analysis will come from the Work 

Sheet; principally columm 3. In some instances, the reviewer will have to selectively summarize more lengthy notes taken in the field on the defective element. In other instances, it may be necessary to relate column 2 to column 3 in order that the defect becomes apparent. In any case, the entries should be clear, brief and specific to facilitate use by State office. (Rev. 6/65)

Manual Citation: The use of this column is optional with the State. reviewer may enter here the citation of the policy involved in the defective action(s). This might be a page number, a section number, or whatever method of reference is used by the State to identify policy provisions in the Mamual.

General: 

V. FACE SHEET 

This is the first page of the work schedule and provides for identifying information about the case as a help to the reviewer. Most of the information necessary to complete this sheet would ordinarily be found in the case record. Any additional information obtained by the reviewer in the course of the investigation may be added to this sheet. 

If the case record contains a complete current face sheet with essentially the same information, the reviewer may obtain a photo-copy of this in place of completing this form. 

1. Living Arrangement: The reviewer will indicate here the type of setting in which the recipient lives; i.e., his own home, nursing home, institution, or other. This need not be a lengthy description, rather, a brief 2. indication to aid the reviewer.

Guardian or Legal Representative: Enter here the name and address of the person, if any, who has been legally designated to act for the recipient, 1.e., guardian, conservator, committee, curator, etc. 

3. Members of the Household: Enter the names of all persons living in the household. This would include both related and unrelated persons who are living as part of the same family unit with the recipient or who are otherwise closely associated with him. It would not include other persons or families who may happen to occupy the same dwelling but have no direct significance for the subject case. The purpose of this section is to provide the reviewer with information which will help him understand the case situation and recognize persons whom he may encounter during the home visit. If additional space is needed, the reverse side of this sheet may be used or lines may be borrowed from another section on the page.

Relationship or 

Birthdates are to be entered in the column provided. significance means the family tie to the recipient or other useful information regarding the persons listed in this section. Initials may be used for relationship, as S(son), D(daughter), GS (grandson), N(niece), etc. The significance of any unrelated persons as well as receipt of any other form of assistance and case number would be noted here. Enter the Social Security number on the same line as the name of the individual to whom it applies. This is especially important (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59 

))

II : 

 (cont.) 

for the recipient, spouse, and any other persons whose entitlement to benefits might have a bearing on the recipient's circumstances. 

If the number cannot be determined, either from the case record or field investigation, enter "Unknown". If the individual never had a 

Social Security number, enter "None". The asterisk (*) column is to be used to indicate whether the persons listed as members of the household are included in the assistance group, i.e., A or not included in the assistance group, i.e., NA. 

4. Significant Persons Not in the Household: 

Enter the names of any other persons, not in the household, of significance to the recipient, i.e., responsible relatives including absent parents and other persons who contribute to the support of the recipient. This would also include the fathers or alleged fathers of unborn children when a member of the assistance group is pregnant. Specify after the name if deceased. If the name of the father of one or more of the children is unknown, signify this by entering "Father Unknown" in the name column.

Age is to be entered in the column provided. The approximate age may be used if the actual age is not known. Relationship or significance is to be used to show useful information regarding the persons listed in this section, such as relationship to the recipient, the nature of the paternal relationship (such as legal, alleged, adjudicated, or acknowledged father) and identify by line number from item 3, the children to which this relationship pertains, the significance of any unrelated persons, the Social Security number if this may be of significance, receipt of any other form of assistance and case number, etc. 

If the Address cannot be determined either from the case record or from field investigation, enter "Unknown". 

5. Other Information of Use to Reviewer: This space may be used to enter any additional information which the reviewer believes may be helpful to him in conducting the field investigation. Examples of such information might be language barriers, ethnic or nationality group, length of time on assistance under this or another category, VA claim number, directions to the client's home, telephone number, etc.

VI. ELEMENTS OF ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION 

General: These pages of the Work Sheet are used by the QC reviewer to record his analysis and evaluation of the adequacy and quality of the local agency's performance in each step of the process of determining eligibility and amount of payment under the State plan. The element numbers in column 1 may be circled to denote a defective element ir to do so would assist the reviewer. Columns 2 and 3 must carry sufficient (Rev. 6/65)

H.T. No. 59

II 

Distr. Form FS-341 - (cont.) 

information on defective elements to permit the reviewer to make his analysis in Part IV "Analysis of Defects in Case Action" of the Schedule 

Proper.

Column 1 Elements of Eligibility Determination 

In this column are listed a number of elements which are known to be common to all or a substantial number of State plans. Definitions of these elements in relation to QC are found on pages II-30 to II-35. States are to add under program area 50, Other any additional State requirements not included herein, e.g., residence, transfer of property, citizenship, school attendance, etc. Column 2 

QC Analysis of Case Record 

The primary purpose of this column is to assist the QC reviewer in planning for the field investigation. The reviewer should bear this purpose in mind and use this column in such a way as will be most helpful to him. Any question raised must be explained in column 2. Any pertinent facts should be noted, together with the methods used by the local agency to establish these facts, and why these facts, methods or conclusions are under question. 

Conflicts in information obtained, factors subject to change, reliability of sources used, and gaps in coverage should be noted. If questions pertain to some, but not to all, persons in the family, these persons should be identified. This colum may also be used selectively to highlight other points for consideration in the field, or to enter information which will serve the reviewer as a reminder of the case situation. 

Column 3Findings of Field Investigation 

In this column the reviewer will record any findings of the field investigation which reveal discrepancies in information or defects in the action of the local agency. It may also be used to note confirming information. 

Any new or different facts obtained by the reviewer and the methods of sources used for obtaining these facts should be shown, as well as any errors by the local agency in applying State policy to the facts at hand or otherwise reaching correct conclusions. 

Note that QC is a review of the validity of the actions of the local agency at the time those actions were taken. Therefore, the entries to be made in this colum will relate only to the facts of the situation as they existed at that time. Changes in circumstances subsequent to the local agency's action would not be regarded as discrepancies and would not be shown. They should, however, be brought to the attention of the local agency by some other method established by the State. (Rev. 6/65)

10. BASIC PROGRAM REQUIREMENTS 

11. Age 12. 

Where age is a factor of eligibility, the reviewer will decide whether the recipient(s) is within the age limit prescribed by the State, and whether this age was established in a manner prescribed by the State agency. 

Institutional Status 

13 

Where the recipient is in an institution, the reviewer will decide whether the State's policy concerning residence in a public or private institution, medical or otherwise, has been properly applied. 

13. Disability; Blindness (APTD or AB only or Title XVI where eligibility is based on either disability or blindness, --Disability The reviewer will decide whether the local agency obtained, completed and submitted to the medical review team all required medical reports and social information prescribed in the State plan and/or requested by the medical review team. This element includes such factors as: whether medical information was current, complete, and from an accepted source; whether changes in circumstances of specified medical care and treatment or in the social situation which affects the individuals ability to function were considered in relation to eligibility, e.g., recent hospitalization, new treatment, physical rehabilitation, training, noticeable changes in ability to perform a useful occupation, pertinent changes in living arrangment, and community employment resources, etc.; and whether additional or follow-up medical or social information requested by the team, as of a specified date was obtained and submitted. 

Also, the reviewer will decide whether there is a record of the medical review team's decision and whether the local agency took the proper action in response to this decision.

Blindness The reviewer will decide whether the local agency obtained and submitted the eye reports and medical examinations prescribed by the State agency and/or requested by the State Supervising Ophthalmologist. This element includes such factors as: whether use was made of a qualified examiner and the information recorded on the prescribed form or its equivalent; whether changes in circumstances were considered in relation to vision, e.g., favorable response to treatment, new eye reports from other (Rev. 6/65)
